(dynamic workflow)","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"design","description":"Grant or revoke Claude agent access to your Design projects","argumentHint":"consent | revoke","builtin":true},{"name":"design-sync","description":"Push a React design system to claude.ai/design. This runs a converter that bundles the real component code (from Storybook or a bare package) and uploads it. Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). usage","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"anthropic-skills:built-in-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses the built-in browser, the browser pane inside the Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"88c054a7-67e3-4d8e-a4cf-e9188ed0ad06","session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752"}

The search_path is\n238\t      // tenant,messaging,public, so an unqualified table can resolve into\n239\t      // the messaging schema; if the same channel id ever lives in another\n240\t      // tenant, the predicate keeps the lookup scoped. RLS\n241\t      // (bindTenantRlsContext) already fences the rows; the predicate is\n242\t      // the second defense layer.\n243\t      const member = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(ChannelMember, {\n244\t        where: {\n245\t          tenantId: data.tenantId,\n246\t          channelId: data.channelId,\n247\t          userId: data.userId,\n248\t          leftAt: IsNull(),\n249\t        },\n250\t      });\n251\t      return !!member;\n252\t    });\n253\t  }\n254\t\n255\t  /**\n256\t   * Returns the list of active members for a given channel.\n257\t   * Used by notification-service to determine push-notification recipients.\n258\t   */\n259\t  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.getChannelMembers')\n260\t  async getChannelMembers(@Payload() data: GetChannelMembersPayload): Promise<ChannelMemberDto[]> {\n261\t    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n262\t      const members = await queryRunner.manager.find(ChannelMember, {\n263\t        where: {\n264\t          channelId: data.channelId,\n265\t          leftAt: IsNull(),\n266\t        },\n267\t      });\n268\t\n269\t      return members.map((m) => ({\n270\t        userId: m.userId,\n271\t        role: m.role,\n272\t        joinedAt: m.joinedAt,\n273\t      }));\n274\t    });\n275\t  }\n276\t\n277\t  /**\n278\t   * Returns messages by IDs — used by ai-service for embedding generation.\n279\t   */\n280\t  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.getMessageBatch')\n281\t  async getMessageBatch(@Payload() data: GetMessageBatchPayload): Promise<MessageBatchDto[]> {\n282\t    if (!data.messageIds || data.messageIds.length === 0) return [];\n283\t\n284\t    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n285\t      const messages = await queryRunner.manager.find(Message, {\n286\t        where: { id: In(data.messageIds), isDeleted: false },\n287\t      });\n288\t\n289\t      return messages.map((m) => ({\n290\t        id: m.id,\n291\t        channelId: m.channelId,\n292\t        senderId: m.senderId,\n293\t        content: m.content,\n294\t        contentType: m.contentType,\n295\t        createdAt: m.createdAt,\n296\t      }));\n297\t    });\n298\t  }\n299\t\n300\t  /**\n301\t   * Hydrate one message into the full {@link WsMessage} the gateway WS bridge\n302\t   * broadcasts (MSG-CRITICAL-050). The NATS domain events are thin (IDs only)\n303\t   * per ADR-006; the bridge cannot emit a body the client can render without\n304\t   * this hydration step.\n305\t   *\n306\t   * SECURITY: the response carries `sender: { id }` ONLY — display PII\n307\t   * (name/avatar) is deliberately NOT exposed over NATS (auth-user-queries\n308\t   * profile-oracle constraint). The client enriches the sender from its\n309\t   * channel-members cache (loaded over the authorized GraphQL federation path).\n310\t   * Attachments carry tenant-scoped presigned URLs (the same isolation guard as\n311\t   * the GraphQL field resolver). Returns `{ message: null }` when the message is\n312\t   * absent in the tenant — the bridge then drops the broadcast.\n313\t   */\n314\t  @MessagePattern(GET_MESSAGE_FOR_BROADCAST_SUBJECT)\n315\t  async getMessageForBroadcast(\n316\t    @Payload() data: GetMessageForBroadcastRequest,\n317\t  ): Promise<GetMessageForBroadcastResponse> {\n318\t    if (\n319\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) ||\n320\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.channelId) ||\n321\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.messageId)\n322\t    ) {\n323\t      this.logger.warn('Rejected getMessageForBroadcast with invalid id format');\n324\t      return { message: null };\n325\t    }\n326\t\n327\t    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n328\t      const message = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(Message, {\n329\t        where: { id: data.messageId, channelId: data.channelId, isDeleted: false },\n330\t      });\n331\t      if (!message) {\n332\t        return { message: null };\n333\t      }\n334\t\n335\t      const [attachments, receipts] = await Promise.all([\n336\t        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageAttachment, {\n337\t          where: {\n338\t            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n339\t            messageId: message.id,\n340\t            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n341\t            isDeleted: false,\n342\t          },\n343\t          order: { createdAt: 'ASC' },\n344\t        }),\n345\t        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageReceipt, {\n346\t          where: {\n347\t            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n348\t            messageId: message.id,\n349\t            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n350\t          },\n351\t          order: { receiptCreatedAt: 'ASC' },\n352\t        }),\n353\t      ]);\n354\t\n355\t      const wsAttachments: WsMessageAttachment[] = await Promise.all(\n356\t        attachments.map(async (a) => ({\n357\t          id: a.id,\n358\t          originalFilename: a.originalFilename,\n359\t          mimeType: a.mimeType,\n360\t          fileSize: Number(a.fileSize),\n361\t          width: a.width,\n362\t          height: a.height,\n363\t          durationSeconds: a.durationSeconds,\n364\t          downloadUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.storageKey),\n365\t          thumbnailUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.thumbnailKey),\n366\t        })),\n367\t      );\n368\t\n369\t      const wsReceipts: WsMessageReceipt[] = receipts.map((r) => ({\n370\t        userId: r.userId,\n371\t        // Project the lowercase DB enum VALUE → UPPERCASE GraphQL enum NAME so\n372\t        // the live WS wire form matches the GraphQL query wire form exactly\n373\t        // (S1-CODEGEN). WsReceiptStatus IS the ReceiptStatus key union.\n374\t        status: toWireEnumName(ReceiptStatus, r.status) satisfies WsReceiptStatus,\n375\t        deliveredAt: r.deliveredAt ? r.deliveredAt.toISOString() : null,\n376\t        readAt: r.readAt ? r.readAt.toISOString() : null,\n377\t      }));\n378\t\n379\t      const wsMessage: WsMessage = {\n380\t        id: message.id,\n381\t        channelId: message.channelId,\n382\t        senderId: message.senderId,\n383\t        content: message.content,\n384\t        // VALUE → NAME projection (see wsReceipts above). WsMessageContentType\n385\t        // IS the MessageContentType key union.\n386\t        contentType: toWireEnumName(\n387\t          MessageContentType,\n388\t          message.contentType,\n389\t        ) satisfies WsMessageContentType,\n390\t        parentId: message.parentId,\n391\t        forwardedFrom: message.forwardedFrom,\n392\t        isDeleted: message.isDeleted,\n393\t        createdAt: message.createdAt.toISOString(),\n394\t        editedAt: message.editedAt ? message.editedAt.toISOString() : null,\n395\t        metadata: message.metadata,\n396\t        idempotencyKey: message.idempotencyKey,\n397\t        sender: { id: message.senderId },\n398\t        attachments: wsAttachments,\n399\t        receipts: wsReceipts,\n400\t      };\n401\t      return { message: wsMessage };\n402\t    });\n403\t  }\n404\t\n405\t  /** Presign a storage key, returning null on absence or a cross-tenant/transient failure. */\n406\t  private async safePresign(tenantId: string, storageKey: string | null): Promise<string | null> {\n407\t    if (!storageKey) {\n408\t      return null;\n409\t    }\n410\t    try {\n411\t      return await this.mediaService.generateDownloadUrl(tenantId, storageKey);\n412\t    } catch (error) {\n413\t      this.logger.warn(`Broadcast presign failed: ${(error as Error).message}`);\n414\t      return null;\n415\t    }\n416\t  }\n417\t\n418\t  /**\n419\t   * Resolve an opaque push notification ref after the mobile client is authenticated.\n420\t   *\n421\t   * Push payloads never carry channelId/messageId. The ref is stored under\n422\t   * tenant+recipient scope and consumed atomically, so wrong tenant/user lookups\n423\t   * miss without burning the real recipient's ref while valid double-click\n424\t   * replays fail after the first successful resolution.\n425\t   */\n426\t  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.resolveNotificationRef')\n427\t  async resolveNotificationRef(\n428\t    @Payload() data: ResolveNotificationRefPayload,\n429\t  ): Promise<ResolveNotificationRefResult | null> {\n430\t    if (\n431\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) ||\n432\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.userId) ||\n433\t      !NOTIFICATION_REF_REGEX.test(data.notificationRef)\n434\t    ) {\n435\t      this.logger.warn('Rejected resolveNotificationRef with invalid tenant/user/ref format');\n436\t      return null;\n437\t    }\n438\t\n439\t    const key = `msg:push:ref:${data.tenantId}:${data.userId}:${data.notificationRef}`;\n440\t    const raw = await this.consumeRedisKey(key);\n441\t    if (!raw) {\n442\t      return null;\n443\t    }\n444\t\n445\t    let record: NotificationRefRecord;\n446\t    try {\n447\t      record = JSON.parse(raw) as NotificationRefRecord;\n448\t    } catch {\n449\t      this.logger.warn('Rejected malformed notificationRef record');\n450\t      return null;\n451\t    }\n452\t\n453\t    if (\n454\t      record.tenantId !== data.tenantId ||\n455\t      record.userId !== data.userId ||\n456\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(record.channelId) ||\n457\t      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(record.messageId)\n458\t    ) {\n459\t      this.logger.warn(\n460\t        'Rejected notificationRef with mismatched tenant/user or invalid target IDs',\n461\t      );\n462\t      return null;\n463\t    }\n464\t\n465\t    const messageCreatedAt = new Date(record.messageCreatedAt);\n466\t    if (Number.isNaN(messageCreatedAt.getTime())) {\n467\t      this.logger.warn('Rejected notificationRef with invalid messageCreatedAt');\n468\t      return null;\n469\t    }\n470\t\n471\t    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n472\t      const member = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(ChannelMember, {\n473\t        where: {\n474\t          channelId: record.channelId,\n475\t          userId: data.userId,\n476\t          leftAt: IsNull(),\n477\t        },\n478\t      });\n479\t      if (!member) {\n480\t        return null;\n481\t      }\n482\t\n483\t      const messageRows: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n484\t        `SELECT EXISTS(\n485\t           SELECT 1\n486\t           FROM messages\n487\t           WHERE id = $1\n488\t             AND \"channelId\" = $2\n489\t             AND \"createdAt\" = $3::timestamptz\n490\t             AND \"isDeleted\" = false\n491\t        ) AS exists`,\n492\t        [record.messageId, record.channelId, record.messageCreatedAt],\n493\t      );\n494\t      if (!booleanColumn(messageRows, 'exists')) {\n495\t        return null;\n496\t      }\n497\t\n498\t      return {\n499\t        channelId: record.channelId,\n500\t        messageId: record.messageId,\n501\t        messageCreatedAt: record.messageCreatedAt,\n502\t      };\n503\t    });\n504\t  }\n505\t\n506\t  /**\n507\t   * Handles user deletion: anonymises messages, removes channel memberships,\n508\t   * and cleans up reactions/receipts in a single transaction.\n509\t   * Pins tenant schema before executing any queries.\n510\t   */\n511\t  @EventPattern('events.*.UserDeleted')\n512\t  async We need the IDs up front to clean AI-derived tables.\n557\t          const userMsgRowsResult: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n558\t            `SELECT id, \"channelId\" FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1`,\n559\t            [deletedUserId],\n560\t          );\n561\t          const userMsgRows = Array.isArray(userMsgRowsResult)\n562\t            ? userMsgRowsResult.filter(isMessageChannelRow)\n563\t            : [];\n564\t          const userMessageIds = userMsgRows.map((r) => r.id);\n565\t\n566\t          // Determine which channels the user has messages in\n567\t          const channelRows = userMsgRows.reduce<Array<{ channelId: string }>>((acc, r) => {\n568\t            if (!acc.some((x) => x.channelId === r.channelId)) {\n569\t              acc.push({ channelId: r.channelId });\n570\t            }\n571\t            return acc;\n572\t          }, []);\n573\t\n574\t          // For each channel, check legal hold status and anonymize accordingly.\n575\t          // BEFORE: all messages wiped unconditionally — messages in litigation-held\n576\t          // channels had content destroyed, creating spoliation liability.\n577\t          // WHY: Legal hold requires content preservation. We still anonymize the senderId\n578\t          // (to protect the user's identity under GDPR) but preserve content in held channels.\n579\t          const heldChannelIds = new Set<string>();\n580\t          for (const { channelId } of channelRows) {\n581\t            const isHeld = await this.legalHoldService.isUnderLegalHold(data.tenantId, channelId);\n582\t            if (isHeld) {\n583\t              heldChannelIds.add(channelId);\n584\t              // Held channel: anonymize sender identity only, preserve content\n585\t              await queryRunner.query(\n586\t                `UPDATE messages SET \"senderId\" = $1 WHERE \"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" = $3`,\n587\t                [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, channelId],\n588\t              );\n589\t            }\n590\t          }\n591\t\n592\t          // MSG-CRITICAL-058: for messages in NON-HELD channels (whose content we are\n593\t          // about to wipe), delete the attachment rows and collect their MinIO object\n594\t          // keys so the binaries are purged after commit. GdprService.anonymizeMyData() correctly\n621\t          // sets embedding=NULL; this handler now aligns with that behavior.\n622\t          if (heldChannelIds.size < channelRows.length) {\n623\t            const heldIds = Array.from(heldChannelIds);\n624\t            const whereClause =\n625\t              heldIds.length > 0\n626\t                ? `\"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" != ALL($3::uuid[])`\n627\t                : `\"senderId\" = $2`;\n628\t            const params =\n629\t              heldIds.length > 0\n630\t                ? [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, heldIds]\n631\t                : [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId];\n632\t\n633\t            await queryRunner.query(\n634\t              `UPDATE messages\n635\t             SET \"senderId\" = $1,\n636\t                 content = '[message deleted by user]',\n637\t                 embedding = NULL\n638\t             WHERE ${whereClause}`,\n639\t              params,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752","uuid":"98cfeafc-6dc9-43cf-83de-7ba13fcb2ae2","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:47:11.266Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-239b9d72de9d/apps/messaging-service/src/event-handlers/messaging-nats.handler.ts","content":"          );\n        }\n      }\n      throw error;\n    } finally {\n      await queryRunner.release();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Verify that a user is an active member of a channel.\n   * Used by the WebSocket gateway for join-room authorisation.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.verifyMembership')\n  async verifyMembership(@Payload() data: VerifyMembershipPayload): Promise<boolean> {\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      // MSGFIX-FAZ1: explicit tenantId predicate (the GraphQL-side pattern —\n      // message.resolver.ts validateChannelMembership). The search_path is\n      // tenant,messaging,public, so an unqualified table can resolve into\n      // the messaging schema; if the same channel id ever lives in another\n      // tenant, the predicate keeps the lookup scoped. RLS\n      // (bindTenantRlsContext) already fences the rows; the predicate is\n      // the second defense layer.\n      const member = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(ChannelMember, {\n        where: {\n          tenantId: data.tenantId,\n          channelId: data.channelId,\n          userId: data.userId,\n          leftAt: IsNull(),\n        },\n      });\n      return !!member;\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Returns the list of active members for a given channel.\n   * Used by notification-service to determine push-notification recipients.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.getChannelMembers')\n  async getChannelMembers(@Payload() data: GetChannelMembersPayload): Promise<ChannelMemberDto[]> {\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const members = await queryRunner.manager.find(ChannelMember, {\n        where: {\n          channelId: data.channelId,\n          leftAt: IsNull(),\n        },\n      });\n\n      return members.map((m) => ({\n        userId: m.userId,\n        role: m.role,\n        joinedAt: m.joinedAt,\n      }));\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Returns messages by IDs — used by ai-service for embedding generation.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.getMessageBatch')\n  async getMessageBatch(@Payload() data: GetMessageBatchPayload): Promise<MessageBatchDto[]> {\n    if (!data.messageIds || data.messageIds.length === 0) return [];\n\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const messages = await queryRunner.manager.find(Message, {\n        where: { id: In(data.messageIds), isDeleted: false },\n      });\n\n      return messages.map((m) => ({\n        id: m.id,\n        channelId: m.channelId,\n        senderId: m.senderId,\n        content: m.content,\n        contentType: m.contentType,\n        createdAt: m.createdAt,\n      }));\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Hydrate one message into the full {@link WsMessage} the gateway WS bridge\n   * broadcasts (MSG-CRITICAL-050). The NATS domain events are thin (IDs only)\n   * per ADR-006; the bridge cannot emit a body the client can render without\n   * this hydration step.\n   *\n   * SECURITY: the response carries `sender: { id }` ONLY — display PII\n   * (name/avatar) is deliberately NOT exposed over NATS (auth-user-queries\n   * profile-oracle constraint). The client enriches the sender from its\n   * channel-members cache (loaded over the authorized GraphQL federation path).\n   * Attachments carry tenant-scoped presigned URLs (the same isolation guard as\n   * the GraphQL field resolver). Returns `{ message: null }` when the message is\n   * absent in the tenant — the bridge then drops the broadcast.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern(GET_MESSAGE_FOR_BROADCAST_SUBJECT)\n  async getMessageForBroadcast(\n    @Payload() data: GetMessageForBroadcastRequest,\n  ): Promise<GetMessageForBroadcastResponse> {\n    if (\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.channelId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.messageId)\n    ) {\n      this.logger.warn('Rejected getMessageForBroadcast with invalid id format');\n      return { message: null };\n    }\n\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const message = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(Message, {\n        where: { id: data.messageId, channelId: data.channelId, isDeleted: false },\n      });\n      if (!message) {\n        return { message: null };\n      }\n\n      const [attachments, receipts] = await Promise.all([\n        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageAttachment, {\n          where: {\n            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n            messageId: message.id,\n            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n            isDeleted: false,\n          },\n          order: { createdAt: 'ASC' },\n        }),\n        queryRunner.manager.find(MessageReceipt, {\n          where: {\n            tenantId: data.tenantId,\n            messageId: message.id,\n            messageCreatedAt: message.createdAt,\n          },\n          order: { receiptCreatedAt: 'ASC' },\n        }),\n      ]);\n\n      const wsAttachments: WsMessageAttachment[] = await Promise.all(\n        attachments.map(async (a) => ({\n          id: a.id,\n          originalFilename: a.originalFilename,\n          mimeType: a.mimeType,\n          fileSize: Number(a.fileSize),\n          width: a.width,\n          height: a.height,\n          durationSeconds: a.durationSeconds,\n          downloadUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.storageKey),\n          thumbnailUrl: await this.safePresign(data.tenantId, a.thumbnailKey),\n        })),\n      );\n\n      const wsReceipts: WsMessageReceipt[] = receipts.map((r) => ({\n        userId: r.userId,\n        // Project the lowercase DB enum VALUE → UPPERCASE GraphQL enum NAME so\n        // the live WS wire form matches the GraphQL query wire form exactly\n        // (S1-CODEGEN). WsReceiptStatus IS the ReceiptStatus key union.\n        status: toWireEnumName(ReceiptStatus, r.status) satisfies WsReceiptStatus,\n        deliveredAt: r.deliveredAt ? r.deliveredAt.toISOString() : null,\n        readAt: r.readAt ? r.readAt.toISOString() : null,\n      }));\n\n      const wsMessage: WsMessage = {\n        id: message.id,\n        channelId: message.channelId,\n        senderId: message.senderId,\n        content: message.content,\n        // VALUE → NAME projection (see wsReceipts above). WsMessageContentType\n        // IS the MessageContentType key union.\n        contentType: toWireEnumName(\n          MessageContentType,\n          message.contentType,\n        ) satisfies WsMessageContentType,\n        parentId: message.parentId,\n        forwardedFrom: message.forwardedFrom,\n        isDeleted: message.isDeleted,\n        createdAt: message.createdAt.toISOString(),\n        editedAt: message.editedAt ? message.editedAt.toISOString() : null,\n        metadata: message.metadata,\n        idempotencyKey: message.idempotencyKey,\n        sender: { id: message.senderId },\n        attachments: wsAttachments,\n        receipts: wsReceipts,\n      };\n      return { message: wsMessage };\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** Presign a storage key, returning null on absence or a cross-tenant/transient failure. */\n  private async safePresign(tenantId: string, storageKey: string | null): Promise<string | null> {\n    if (!storageKey) {\n      return null;\n    }\n    try {\n      return await this.mediaService.generateDownloadUrl(tenantId, storageKey);\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.logger.warn(`Broadcast presign failed: ${(error as Error).message}`);\n      return null;\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Resolve an opaque push notification ref after the mobile client is authenticated.\n   *\n   * Push payloads never carry channelId/messageId. The ref is stored under\n   * tenant+recipient scope and consumed atomically, so wrong tenant/user lookups\n   * miss without burning the real recipient's ref while valid double-click\n   * replays fail after the first successful resolution.\n   */\n  @MessagePattern('request.messaging.resolveNotificationRef')\n  async resolveNotificationRef(\n    @Payload() data: ResolveNotificationRefPayload,\n  ): Promise<ResolveNotificationRefResult | null> {\n    if (\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.userId) ||\n      !NOTIFICATION_REF_REGEX.test(data.notificationRef)\n    ) {\n      this.logger.warn('Rejected resolveNotificationRef with invalid tenant/user/ref format');\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    const key = `msg:push:ref:${data.tenantId}:${data.userId}:${data.notificationRef}`;\n    const raw = await this.consumeRedisKey(key);\n    if (!raw) {\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    let record: NotificationRefRecord;\n    try {\n      record = JSON.parse(raw) as NotificationRefRecord;\n    } catch {\n      this.logger.warn('Rejected malformed notificationRef record');\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    if (\n      record.tenantId !== data.tenantId ||\n      record.userId !== data.userId ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(record.channelId) ||\n      !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(record.messageId)\n    ) {\n      this.logger.warn(\n        'Rejected notificationRef with mismatched tenant/user or invalid target IDs',\n      );\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    const messageCreatedAt = new Date(record.messageCreatedAt);\n    if (Number.isNaN(messageCreatedAt.getTime())) {\n      this.logger.warn('Rejected notificationRef with invalid messageCreatedAt');\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    return this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => {\n      const member = await queryRunner.manager.findOne(ChannelMember, {\n        where: {\n          channelId: record.channelId,\n          userId: data.userId,\n          leftAt: IsNull(),\n        },\n      });\n      if (!member) {\n        return null;\n      }\n\n      const messageRows: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n        `SELECT EXISTS(\n           SELECT 1\n           FROM messages\n           WHERE id = $1\n             AND \"channelId\" = $2\n             AND \"createdAt\" = $3::timestamptz\n             AND \"isDeleted\" = false\n        ) AS exists`,\n        [record.messageId, record.channelId, record.messageCreatedAt],\n      );\n      if (!booleanColumn(messageRows, 'exists')) {\n        return null;\n      }\n\n      return {\n        channelId: record.channelId,\n        messageId: record.messageId,\n        messageCreatedAt: record.messageCreatedAt,\n      };\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Handles user deletion: anonymises messages, removes channel memberships,\n   * and cleans up reactions/receipts in a single transaction.\n   * Pins tenant schema before executing any queries.\n   */\n  @EventPattern('events.*.UserDeleted')\n  async handleUserDeleted(@Payload() data: UserDeletedPayload): Promise<void> {\n    const deletedUserId = data.deletedUserId;\n    if (!TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId) || !TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(deletedUserId)) {\n      this.logger.error(\n        'Rejected UserDeleted event missing canonical deletedUserId or valid tenantId',\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n\n    this.logger.log(\n      `Processing UserDeleted for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId} in tenant ${data.tenantId}`,\n    );\n\n    try {\n      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: attachment MinIO object keys collected inside the tx for\n      // the erased (non-held) messages; purged AFTER commit so the DB row is already\n      // gone before we drop the binary (a failed purge leaves an orphan, not a live\n      // PII reference). Held channels are excluded — legal hold preserves their media.\n      const objectKeysToPurge = await this.withTenantQueryRunner(\n        data.tenantId,\n        async (queryRunner) => {\n          // SECURITY: Verify user has actual presence in claimed tenant before destructive cascade\n          const userMessages: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_messages`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n          const userMemberships: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM channel_members WHERE \"userId\" = $1 LIMIT 1) AS has_memberships`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n\n          if (\n            !booleanColumn(userMessages, 'has_messages') &&\n            !booleanColumn(userMemberships, 'has_memberships')\n          ) {\n            this.logger.log(\n              `UserDeleted: deletedUserId=${deletedUserId} has no messaging footprint in tenant ${data.tenantId}, skipping cascade`,\n            );\n            return [];\n          }\n\n          // Collect ALL message IDs for this user BEFORE any anonymization.\n          // WHY: after we set senderId=ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, we can no longer identify\n          // which messages belonged to this specific user — ANONYMOUS_USER_ID is shared\n          // across all deleted users. We need the IDs up front to clean AI-derived tables.\n          const userMsgRowsResult: unknown = await queryRunner.query(\n            `SELECT id, \"channelId\" FROM messages WHERE \"senderId\" = $1`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n          const userMsgRows = Array.isArray(userMsgRowsResult)\n            ? userMsgRowsResult.filter(isMessageChannelRow)\n            : [];\n          const userMessageIds = userMsgRows.map((r) => r.id);\n\n          // Determine which channels the user has messages in\n          const channelRows = userMsgRows.reduce<Array<{ channelId: string }>>((acc, r) => {\n            if (!acc.some((x) => x.channelId === r.channelId)) {\n              acc.push({ channelId: r.channelId });\n            }\n            return acc;\n          }, []);\n\n          // For each channel, check legal hold status and anonymize accordingly.\n          // BEFORE: all messages wiped unconditionally — messages in litigation-held\n          // channels had content destroyed, creating spoliation liability.\n          // WHY: Legal hold requires content preservation. We still anonymize the senderId\n          // (to protect the user's identity under GDPR) but preserve content in held channels.\n          const heldChannelIds = new Set<string>();\n          for (const { channelId } of channelRows) {\n            const isHeld = await this.legalHoldService.isUnderLegalHold(data.tenantId, channelId);\n            if (isHeld) {\n              heldChannelIds.add(channelId);\n              // Held channel: anonymize sender identity only, preserve content\n              await queryRunner.query(\n                `UPDATE messages SET \"senderId\" = $1 WHERE \"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" = $3`,\n                [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, channelId],\n              );\n            }\n          }\n\n          // MSG-CRITICAL-058: for messages in NON-HELD channels (whose content we are\n          // about to wipe), delete the attachment rows and collect their MinIO object\n          // keys so the binaries are purged after commit. Held-channel attachments are\n          // preserved — legal hold outranks erasure. Mirrors GdprService.anonymizeMyData.\n          const erasableMessageIds = userMsgRows\n            .filter((r) => !heldChannelIds.has(r.channelId))\n            .map((r) => r.id);\n          const objectKeys: string[] = [];\n          if (erasableMessageIds.length > 0) {\n            const attachmentRows: Array<{ storageKey: string; thumbnailKey: string | null }> =\n              await queryRunner.query(\n                `SELECT \"storageKey\", \"thumbnailKey\" FROM message_attachments\n               WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n                [erasableMessageIds],\n              );\n            for (const row of attachmentRows) {\n              if (row.storageKey) objectKeys.push(row.storageKey);\n              if (row.thumbnailKey) objectKeys.push(row.thumbnailKey);\n            }\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `DELETE FROM message_attachments WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n              [erasableMessageIds],\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Non-held channels: anonymize sender + wipe content + clear embedding.\n          // BEFORE: embedding column was NOT cleared — vector index retained the user's\n          // original message content even after anonymization, enabling re-identification\n          // via semantic similarity search. GdprService.anonymizeMyData() correctly\n          // sets embedding=NULL; this handler now aligns with that behavior.\n          if (heldChannelIds.size < channelRows.length) {\n            const heldIds = Array.from(heldChannelIds);\n            const whereClause =\n              heldIds.length > 0\n                ? `\"senderId\" = $2 AND \"channelId\" != ALL($3::uuid[])`\n                : `\"senderId\" = $2`;\n            const params =\n              heldIds.length > 0\n                ? [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId, heldIds]\n                : [ANONYMOUS_USER_ID, deletedUserId];\n\n            await queryRunner.query(\n              `UPDATE messages\n             SET \"senderId\" = $1,\n                 content = '[message deleted by user]',\n                 embedding = NULL\n             WHERE ${whereClause}`,\n              params,","numLines":420,"startLine":220,"totalLines":764}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VeD9ZA8oN9g5vGwPtqJFyA","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\n2\timport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n3\timport { applyTenantRlsToSchema, removeTenantRlsFromSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/database';\n4\t\n5\t/**\n6\t * Migration: Install canonical tenant_isolation_policy on all tenant-scoped\n7\t * messaging tables.\n8\t *\n9\t * # Why this exists — ADR-011 convergence (2026-04-14 plan)\n10\t *\n11\t * Before this migration, messaging was the only platform service whose\n12\t * source-schema tables had NO Row-Level Security policies. Tenant\n13\t * isolation relied solely on search_path mutation in\n14\t * TenantSchemaMiddleware — a single forgotten SET search_path in a\n15\t * handler (or a connection leaking unset GUCs) would cross tenant\n16\t * boundaries silently.\n17\t *\n18\t * This migration closes that gap by installing the canonical\n19\t * `tenant_isolation_policy` (via backend-common's\n20\t * `applyTenantRlsToSchema`) on every tenant-scoped messaging table —\n21\t * the same helper used by farm / sensor / hr / auth / etc. RLS is now\n22\t * the PRIMARY isolation mechanism; search_path remains as defense in\n23\t * depth.\n24\t *\n25\t * # Partition handling\n26\t *\n27\t * `messages` and `message_receipts` are RANGE-partitioned by time.\n28\t * PostgreSQL's RLS semantics automatically apply a policy created on\n29\t * the partition parent to every existing and future partition, so the\n30\t * helper only needs to install on the parent table. The helper's\n31\t * discovery query includes partition children (they have `tenantId`\n32\t * columns too), which is benign — the policy is idempotently\n33\t * re-installed on each partition with the same predicate.\n34\t *\n35\t * # Excludes\n36\t *\n37\t * - `embeddings_metadata` — platform-wide reference data (tracks AI\n38\t *   embedding model versions); not tenant-scoped. Treated like\n39\t *   `audit_logs` / `user_permissions`.\n40\t * - `messaging_outbox` — cross-tenant background worker reads all\n41\t *   tenants' events. Worker runs under `BypassRlsService.withBypass()`\n42\t *   when publishing. Excluded here so application-layer writers still\n43\t *   pass (they already filter by tenantId via entity code; no RLS\n44\t *   needed on the outbox for writers).\n45\t *\n46\t * # Per-tenant schema handling\n47\t *\n48\t * Messaging uses schema-per-tenant clones (`tenant_<uuid>.channels`,\n49\t * etc.) created via `CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL` which does NOT\n50\t * copy RLS policies. The already-wired `RlsModule.forPoolService({\n51\t * syncTenantSchemas: true })` in app.module.ts runs\n52\t * `TenantRlsSyncService` at every service start, which iterates every\n53\t * `tenant_<uuid>` schema and re-applies the same policy via the same\n54\t * helper. This migration + that service together make RLS complete\n55\t * across source + tenant schemas.\n56\t *\n57\t * # Idempotency\n58\t *\n59\t * `applyTenantRlsToSchema` drops the policy by canonical name and\n60\t * recreates it — safe to re-run. Operators can ship predicate changes\n61\t * in a follow-up migration that just calls this helper again with\n62\t * updated options.\n63\t *\n64\t * # Closes findings\n65\t *\n66\t * - docs/reviews/messaging-expert/2026-04-14-tenant-isolation-violation-e2e.md#CRITICAL-MSG-001\n67\t */\n68\texport class EnableRowLevelSecurity1782400000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n69\t  name = 'EnableRowLevelSecurity1782400000000';\n70\t\n71\t  private readonly logger = new Logger(this.name);\n72\t\n73\t  async up(queryRunner: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n74\t    // Defensive: pin search_path to messaging source schema before the\n75\t    // helper runs current_schema()-based discovery. The\n76\t    // MessagingMigrationRunnerService already pins this between every\n77\t    // migration; the explicit SET here keeps the migration correct\n78\t    // under ad-hoc CLI execution too.\n79\t    await queryRunner.query(`SET search_path TO \"messaging\", public`);\n80\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752","uuid":"42382ce3-1339-49b8-b6d9-9df5afd72c38","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:48:29.015Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-239b9d72de9d/apps/messaging-service/src/migrations/.archive/2026-05-18T09-42-38-476Z/1782400000000-EnableRowLevelSecurity.ts","content":"import { MigrationInterface, QueryRunner } from 'typeorm';\nimport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { applyTenantRlsToSchema, removeTenantRlsFromSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/database';\n\n/**\n * Migration: Install canonical tenant_isolation_policy on all tenant-scoped\n * messaging tables.\n *\n * # Why this exists — ADR-011 convergence (2026-04-14 plan)\n *\n * Before this migration, messaging was the only platform service whose\n * source-schema tables had NO Row-Level Security policies. Tenant\n * isolation relied solely on search_path mutation in\n * TenantSchemaMiddleware — a single forgotten SET search_path in a\n * handler (or a connection leaking unset GUCs) would cross tenant\n * boundaries silently.\n *\n * This migration closes that gap by installing the canonical\n * `tenant_isolation_policy` (via backend-common's\n * `applyTenantRlsToSchema`) on every tenant-scoped messaging table —\n * the same helper used by farm / sensor / hr / auth / etc. RLS is now\n * the PRIMARY isolation mechanism; search_path remains as defense in\n * depth.\n *\n * # Partition handling\n *\n * `messages` and `message_receipts` are RANGE-partitioned by time.\n * PostgreSQL's RLS semantics automatically apply a policy created on\n * the partition parent to every existing and future partition, so the\n * helper only needs to install on the parent table. The helper's\n * discovery query includes partition children (they have `tenantId`\n * columns too), which is benign — the policy is idempotently\n * re-installed on each partition with the same predicate.\n *\n * # Excludes\n *\n * - `embeddings_metadata` — platform-wide reference data (tracks AI\n *   embedding model versions); not tenant-scoped. Treated like\n *   `audit_logs` / `user_permissions`.\n * - `messaging_outbox` — cross-tenant background worker reads all\n *   tenants' events. Worker runs under `BypassRlsService.withBypass()`\n *   when publishing. Excluded here so application-layer writers still\n *   pass (they already filter by tenantId via entity code; no RLS\n *   needed on the outbox for writers).\n *\n * # Per-tenant schema handling\n *\n * Messaging uses schema-per-tenant clones (`tenant_<uuid>.channels`,\n * etc.) created via `CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL` which does NOT\n * copy RLS policies. The already-wired `RlsModule.forPoolService({\n * syncTenantSchemas: true })` in app.module.ts runs\n * `TenantRlsSyncService` at every service start, which iterates every\n * `tenant_<uuid>` schema and re-applies the same policy via the same\n * helper. This migration + that service together make RLS complete\n * across source + tenant schemas.\n *\n * # Idempotency\n *\n * `applyTenantRlsToSchema` drops the policy by canonical name and\n * recreates it — safe to re-run. Operators can ship predicate changes\n * in a follow-up migration that just calls this helper again with\n * updated options.\n *\n * # Closes findings\n *\n * - docs/reviews/messaging-expert/2026-04-14-tenant-isolation-violation-e2e.md#CRITICAL-MSG-001\n */\nexport class EnableRowLevelSecurity1782400000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n  name = 'EnableRowLevelSecurity1782400000000';\n\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(this.name);\n\n  async up(queryRunner: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n    // Defensive: pin search_path to messaging source schema before the\n    // helper runs current_schema()-based discovery. The\n    // MessagingMigrationRunnerService already pins this between every\n    // migration; the explicit SET here keeps the migration correct\n    // under ad-hoc CLI execution too.\n    await queryRunner.query(`SET search_path TO \"messaging\", public`);\n","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":108}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SN27QFqorq2yUDaNmPbgkb","type":"tool_result","content":"330\t\n331\t    // Rate limiting\n332\t    ThrottlerModule,\n333\t\n334\t    // Feature modules\n335\t    HealthModule,\n336\t    ChannelModule,\n337\t    MessageModule,\n338\t    PresenceModule,\n339\t    PartitionModule,\n340\t    MessagingOutboxModule,\n341\t    TenantErasureTargetModule.forService('messaging-service'),\n342\t    GdprModule,\n343\t    ComplianceModule,\n344\t    EventHandlersModule,\n345\t    AiModule,\n346\t    MessagingNotificationModule,\n347\t    MetricsModule,\n348\t\n349\t    /**\n350\t     * SECURITY (HIGH-004): Tenant Row-Level Security (schema-per-tenant).\n351\t     * Messages carry user/tenant PII — defence-in-depth isolation via RLS\n352\t     * means an app-layer tenantId bypass cannot read cross-tenant messages.\n353\t     *\n354\t     * Tenant schema RLS DDL is owned by db-migrate/provisioner. Runtime\n355\t     * services do not hold DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY, so registering\n356\t     * TenantRlsSyncService in app boot would fail closed by design.\n357\t     */\n358\t    RlsModule.forPoolService({\n359\t      serviceName: 'messaging',\n360\t      syncTenantSchemas: runtimeTenantRlsSyncEnabled,\n361\t      // See P4 migration docblock for rationale:\n362\t      // - messaging_outbox: cross-tenant worker reads (BypassRls)\n363\t      // - embeddings_metadata: platform-wide reference data (no tenantId)\n364\t      // - message_send_idempotency: source-schema idempotency ledger\n365\t      excludeTables: getRlsExcludeTablesForService('messaging'),\n366\t      tenantIdColumns: ['tenantId'],\n367\t    }),\n368\t    /** P11 of 2026-04-14 teardown — runtime schema-drift validator. */\n369\t    // Tenant execution context interceptor (SSoT registration) — keeps the\n370\t    // validated tenant schema in AsyncLocalStorage across Apollo/CQRS async\n371\t    // boundaries so per-tenant search_path routing holds at pg checkout.\n372\t    TenantExecutionContextModule,\n373\t    // Shared tenant schema-existence cache + TenantProvisioned invalidation\n374\t    // (no stale-negative-cache block for freshly provisioned tenants)."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752","uuid":"822db7e3-3cd8-46ae-9aba-02ff1a377892","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:48:39.234Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-239b9d72de9d/apps/messaging-service/src/app.module.ts","content":"\n    // Rate limiting\n    ThrottlerModule,\n\n    // Feature modules\n    HealthModule,\n    ChannelModule,\n    MessageModule,\n    PresenceModule,\n    PartitionModule,\n    MessagingOutboxModule,\n    TenantErasureTargetModule.forService('messaging-service'),\n    GdprModule,\n    ComplianceModule,\n    EventHandlersModule,\n    AiModule,\n    MessagingNotificationModule,\n    MetricsModule,\n\n    /**\n     * SECURITY (HIGH-004): Tenant Row-Level Security (schema-per-tenant).\n     * Messages carry user/tenant PII — defence-in-depth isolation via RLS\n     * means an app-layer tenantId bypass cannot read cross-tenant messages.\n     *\n     * Tenant schema RLS DDL is owned by db-migrate/provisioner. Runtime\n     * services do not hold DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY, so registering\n     * TenantRlsSyncService in app boot would fail closed by design.\n     */\n    RlsModule.forPoolService({\n      serviceName: 'messaging',\n      syncTenantSchemas: runtimeTenantRlsSyncEnabled,\n      // See P4 migration docblock for rationale:\n      // - messaging_outbox: cross-tenant worker reads (BypassRls)\n      // - embeddings_metadata: platform-wide reference data (no tenantId)\n      // - message_send_idempotency: source-schema idempotency ledger\n      excludeTables: getRlsExcludeTablesForService('messaging'),\n      tenantIdColumns: ['tenantId'],\n    }),\n    /** P11 of 2026-04-14 teardown — runtime schema-drift validator. */\n    // Tenant execution context interceptor (SSoT registration) — keeps the\n    // validated tenant schema in AsyncLocalStorage across Apollo/CQRS async\n    // boundaries so per-tenant search_path routing holds at pg checkout.\n    TenantExecutionContextModule,\n    // Shared tenant schema-existence cache + TenantProvisioned invalidation\n    // (no stale-negative-cache block for freshly provisioned tenants).","numLines":45,"startLine":330,"totalLines":446}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E9rJHMpTtA9y14B7XqJ5Td","type":"tool_result","content":"20\t * 3. **Column-name flexibility**: the platform mixes naming conventions —\n21\t *    `farm-service.BaseEntity` uses `tenant_id` (snake_case), while\n22\t *    `auth-service.User` uses `\"tenantId\"` (camelCase quoted). Per-table\n23\t *    discovery handles both.\n24\t * 4. **Bypass clause** baked in: admin-api-service, background workers, and\n25\t *    cross-tenant analytics queries set `app.bypass_rls = 'on'` for the\n26\t *    duration of a transaction. The policy honours this without needing a\n27\t *    separate DB role (which would be operationally heavier).\n28\t * 5. **Idempotent**: safe to re-run after partial failures or in dev/staging\n29\t *    where the schema may already have policies installed (DROP IF EXISTS +\n30\t *    CREATE).\n31\t *\n32\t * # The RLS predicate (security-critical — read carefully)\n33\t *\n34\t * ```sql\n35\t * USING (\n36\t *   -- (1) Explicit bypass for SUPER_ADMIN/background-job contexts.\n37\t *   --     The caller must SET LOCAL app.bypass_rls = 'on' inside a\n38\t *   --     transaction; the setting is reset on COMMIT/ROLLBACK so it\n39\t *   --     cannot leak into pooled connections.\n40\t *   current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on'\n41\t *\n42\t *   OR\n43\t *\n44\t *   -- (2) Tenant context active and matches the row's tenant.\n45\t *   --     NULLIF turns the empty string (when the GUC is unset) into NULL,\n46\t *   --     which makes the cast safe and forces the comparison to UNKNOWN —\n47\t *   --     so no rows are exposed by accident.\n48\t *   \"<tenant_col>\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid\n49\t * )\n50\t * ```\n51\t *\n52\t * Behaviour matrix:\n53\t *\n54\t * | bypass GUC | tenant GUC      | Result                                |\n55\t * | ---------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------- |\n56\t * | unset      | unset           | No rows visible (deny by default)     |\n57\t * | unset      | UUID            | Only rows where tenantId matches      |\n58\t * | 'on'       | unset           | All rows visible (admin/job mode)     |\n59\t * | 'on'       | UUID            | All rows visible (bypass wins)        |\n60\t *\n61\t * # Discovery semantics\n62\t *\n63\t * The helper introspects `information_schema.columns` for the *current*\n64\t * schema and finds every base table that contains a column whose name is in\n65\t * `tenantIdColumns`. Each match gets RLS enabled, FORCED (so even the table\n66\t * owner is subject to the policy), and a `tenant_isolation_policy` created.\n67\t *\n68\t * Tables in `excludeTables` are skipped — typically outbox, audit logs, and\n69\t * any deliberately cross-tenant infrastructure tables.\n70\t *\n71\t * # Why FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY?\n72\t *\n73\t * Without FORCE, the table owner bypasses all policies — and the application\n74\t * connects as the table owner (`aquaculture` user per init-schemas.sql). FORCE\n75\t * removes that escape hatch and is therefore mandatory for defence-in-depth.\n76\t *\n77\t * @example\n78\t * ```ts\n79\t * import { applyTenantRlsToSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/rls';\n80\t *\n81\t * export class EnableRowLevelSecurity1781000000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n82\t *   public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n83\t *     await applyTenantRlsToSchema(qr, {\n84\t *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox', 'audit_logs'],\n85\t *     });\n86\t *   }\n87\t *\n88\t *   public async down(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n89\t *     await removeTenantRlsFromSchema(qr);"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752","uuid":"b961868d-0994-4313-be6e-cbbb5cd4dfd3","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:49:21.947Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-239b9d72de9d/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts","content":" * 3. **Column-name flexibility**: the platform mixes naming conventions —\n *    `farm-service.BaseEntity` uses `tenant_id` (snake_case), while\n *    `auth-service.User` uses `\"tenantId\"` (camelCase quoted). Per-table\n *    discovery handles both.\n * 4. **Bypass clause** baked in: admin-api-service, background workers, and\n *    cross-tenant analytics queries set `app.bypass_rls = 'on'` for the\n *    duration of a transaction. The policy honours this without needing a\n *    separate DB role (which would be operationally heavier).\n * 5. **Idempotent**: safe to re-run after partial failures or in dev/staging\n *    where the schema may already have policies installed (DROP IF EXISTS +\n *    CREATE).\n *\n * # The RLS predicate (security-critical — read carefully)\n *\n * ```sql\n * USING (\n *   -- (1) Explicit bypass for SUPER_ADMIN/background-job contexts.\n *   --     The caller must SET LOCAL app.bypass_rls = 'on' inside a\n *   --     transaction; the setting is reset on COMMIT/ROLLBACK so it\n *   --     cannot leak into pooled connections.\n *   current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on'\n *\n *   OR\n *\n *   -- (2) Tenant context active and matches the row's tenant.\n *   --     NULLIF turns the empty string (when the GUC is unset) into NULL,\n *   --     which makes the cast safe and forces the comparison to UNKNOWN —\n *   --     so no rows are exposed by accident.\n *   \"<tenant_col>\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid\n * )\n * ```\n *\n * Behaviour matrix:\n *\n * | bypass GUC | tenant GUC      | Result                                |\n * | ---------- | --------------- | ------------------------------------- |\n * | unset      | unset           | No rows visible (deny by default)     |\n * | unset      | UUID            | Only rows where tenantId matches      |\n * | 'on'       | unset           | All rows visible (admin/job mode)     |\n * | 'on'       | UUID            | All rows visible (bypass wins)        |\n *\n * # Discovery semantics\n *\n * The helper introspects `information_schema.columns` for the *current*\n * schema and finds every base table that contains a column whose name is in\n * `tenantIdColumns`. Each match gets RLS enabled, FORCED (so even the table\n * owner is subject to the policy), and a `tenant_isolation_policy` created.\n *\n * Tables in `excludeTables` are skipped — typically outbox, audit logs, and\n * any deliberately cross-tenant infrastructure tables.\n *\n * # Why FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY?\n *\n * Without FORCE, the table owner bypasses all policies — and the application\n * connects as the table owner (`aquaculture` user per init-schemas.sql). FORCE\n * removes that escape hatch and is therefore mandatory for defence-in-depth.\n *\n * @example\n * ```ts\n * import { applyTenantRlsToSchema } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/rls';\n *\n * export class EnableRowLevelSecurity1781000000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n *   public async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n *     await applyTenantRlsToSchema(qr, {\n *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox', 'audit_logs'],\n *     });\n *   }\n *\n *   public async down(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n *     await removeTenantRlsFromSchema(qr);","numLines":70,"startLine":20,"totalLines":623}}}

A failed purge orphans the object for a reaper — it does\n682\t      // not leave a live, resolvable PII reference.\n683\t      if (objectKeysToPurge.length > 0) {\n684\t        const purge = await this.attachmentObjectPurge.purgeObjects(\n685\t          data.tenantId,\n686\t          objectKeysToPurge,\n687\t        );\n688\t        if (purge.failed > 0) {\n689\t          this.logger.error(\n690\t            `UserDeleted erasure: ${purge.failed}/${purge.requested} attachment object(s) failed ` +\n691\t              `to delete for tenant ${data.tenantId}; orphaned objects require reaper cleanup`,\n692\t          );\n693\t        }\n694\t      }\n695\t      this.logger.log(`UserDeleted cascade completed for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId}`);\n696\t    } catch (err: unknown) {\n697\t      this.logger.error(`UserDeleted cascade failed for ${deletedUserId}: ${errorMessage(err)}`);\n698\t    }\n699\t  }\n700\t\n701\t  /**\n702\t   * SEC-M17: When a new tenant is provisioned, create messaging partitions for its schema.\n703\t   *\n704\t   * Validates both tenantId (UUID) and schemaName (canonical tenant schema) from the\n705\t   * NATS payload before processing. message_analysis WHERE \"messageId\" = ANY($1::uuid[])`,\n              [userMessageIds],\n            );\n          }\n\n          // Remove reactions\n          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_reactions WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n            deletedUserId,\n          ]);\n\n          // Remove receipts\n          await queryRunner.query(`DELETE FROM message_receipts WHERE \"userId\" = $1`, [\n            deletedUserId,\n          ]);\n\n          // Mark channel memberships as left\n          await queryRunner.query(\n            `UPDATE channel_members SET \"leftAt\" = NOW() WHERE \"userId\" = $1 AND \"leftAt\" IS NULL`,\n            [deletedUserId],\n          );\n\n          return objectKeys;\n        },\n      );\n\n      // MSG-CRITICAL-058: the attachment ROWS are committed-deleted; purge the MinIO\n      // binaries (best-effort, post-commit). Tenant-prefix isolation is enforced\n      // inside purgeObjects. A failed purge orphans the object for a reaper — it does\n      // not leave a live, resolvable PII reference.\n      if (objectKeysToPurge.length > 0) {\n        const purge = await this.attachmentObjectPurge.purgeObjects(\n          data.tenantId,\n          objectKeysToPurge,\n        );\n        if (purge.failed > 0) {\n          this.logger.error(\n            `UserDeleted erasure: ${purge.failed}/${purge.requested} attachment object(s) failed ` +\n              `to delete for tenant ${data.tenantId}; orphaned objects require reaper cleanup`,\n          );\n        }\n      }\n      this.logger.log(`UserDeleted cascade completed for deletedUserId ${deletedUserId}`);\n    } catch (err: unknown) {\n      this.logger.error(`UserDeleted cascade failed for ${deletedUserId}: ${errorMessage(err)}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * SEC-M17: When a new tenant is provisioned, create messaging partitions for its schema.\n   *\n   * Validates both tenantId (UUID) and schemaName (canonical tenant schema) from the\n   * NATS payload before processing. Rejects payloads with invalid formats to prevent\n   * SQL injection via crafted NATS messages from compromised containers.\n   *\n   * Subject shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-317 remediation): the publisher emits the\n   * canonical 3-segment `events.{tenantId}.TenantProvisioned`\n   * (NatsEventBus.deriveSubject). The previous 2-segment literal\n   * `events.TenantProvisioned` could never match a 3-segment publish (NATS\n   * matching is segment-exact — the ORPHAN-013 drift class), so this handler\n   * had NEVER received a TenantProvisioned event.\n   */\n  @EventPattern('events.*.TenantProvisioned')\n  async handleTenantProvisioned(@Payload() data: TenantProvisionedPayload): Promise<void> {\n    // SEC-M17: Validate tenantId format\n    if (!TENANT_ID_REGEX.test(data.tenantId)) {\n      this.logger.error(\n        `SEC-M17: Rejected TenantProvisioned with invalid tenantId: ${String(data.tenantId).substring(0, 50)}`,\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n\n    // SEC-M17: Validate schemaName format and tenant/schema binding.\n    if (!TENANT_SCHEMA_REGEX.test(data.schemaName)) {\n      this.logger.error(\n        `SEC-M17: Rejected TenantProvisioned with invalid schemaName: ${String(data.schemaName).substring(0, 80)}`,\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n    const expectedSchemaName = getTenantSchemaName(data.tenantId);\n    if (data.schemaName !== expectedSchemaName) {\n      this.logger.error(\n        `SEC-M17: Rejected TenantProvisioned with tenant/schema mismatch: ${data.tenantId}`,\n      );\n      return;\n    }\n\n    this.logger.log(`TenantProvisioned received — ensuring partitions for ${data.schemaName}`);\n\n    try {\n      // Trigger partition creation via the partition manager's startup logic\n      // which checks current + next 2 months\n      await this.partitionManager.onApplicationBootstrap();\n      this.logger.log(`Partitions ensured for tenant ${data.tenantId} (${data.schemaName})`);\n    } catch (err) {\n      this.logger.error(`Partition creation failed for ${data.schemaName}: ${errorMessage(err)}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  private async consumeRedisKey(key: string): Promise<string | null> {\n    const result = await this.redis.multi().get(key).del(key).exec();\n    if (!result) return null;\n\n    const [getResult] = result;\n    const [err, value] = getResult ?? We need both because the application\n526\t    // connects as the schema owner (`aquaculture`).\n527\t    await qr.query(\n528\t      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ENABLE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n529\t    );\n530\t    await qr.query(\n531\t      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" FORCE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n532\t    );\n533\t\n534\t    // Step 2: drop any pre-existing policy with the canonical name. This is\n535\t    // what makes the helper a forward-migration tool — predicate changes\n536\t    // (like the NULLIF fix) are applied by simply re-running the helper.\n537\t    await qr.query(\n538\t      `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n539\t        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`,\n540\t    );\n541\t\n542\t    // Step 3: create the policy with the canonical bypass-aware predicate.\n543\t    // FOR ALL covers SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — for INSERT and UPDATE the\n544\t    // USING clause acts as the WITH CHECK clause too, so writers cannot\n545\t    // insert rows for other tenants either.\n546\t    const usingClause = buildTenantPolicyUsingClause(tenantColumn);\n547\t    await qr.query(\n548\t      `${SQL_CREATE_POLICY} \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n549\t        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ` +\n550\t        `FOR ALL ` +\n551\t        `USING ${usingClause} ` +\n552\t        `WITH CHECK ${usingClause}`,\n553\t    );\n554\t\n555\t    logger.log(\n556\t      `RLS armed on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (col: ${tenantColumn})`,\n557\t    );\n558\t    applied++;\n559\t  }"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a582a35e-4944-49fa-949b-9a7532bbd752","uuid":"d170d8ee-df74-4ec4-8f58-ec5338ca1e5d","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:51:58.902Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-239b9d72de9d/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts","content":"          `isolation remains the enforcement boundary for this table.`,\n      );\n      continue;\n    }\n    // Step 1: ENABLE then FORCE RLS. ENABLE turns it on for non-owners, FORCE\n    // extends it to the table owner. We need both because the application\n    // connects as the schema owner (`aquaculture`).\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ENABLE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n    );\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_ALTER_TABLE} \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" FORCE ${SQL_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY}`,\n    );\n\n    // Step 2: drop any pre-existing policy with the canonical name. This is\n    // what makes the helper a forward-migration tool — predicate changes\n    // (like the NULLIF fix) are applied by simply re-running the helper.\n    await qr.query(\n      `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`,\n    );\n\n    // Step 3: create the policy with the canonical bypass-aware predicate.\n    // FOR ALL covers SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE — for INSERT and UPDATE the\n    // USING clause acts as the WITH CHECK clause too, so writers cannot\n    // insert rows for other tenants either.\n    const usingClause = buildTenantPolicyUsingClause(tenantColumn);\n    await qr.query(\n      `${SQL_CREATE_POLICY} \"${TENANT_ISOLATION_POLICY_NAME}\" ` +\n        `ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" ` +\n        `FOR ALL ` +\n        `USING ${usingClause} ` +\n        `WITH CHECK ${usingClause}`,\n    );\n\n    logger.log(\n      `RLS armed on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (col: ${tenantColumn})`,\n    );\n    applied++;\n  }","numLines":40,"startLine":520,"totalLines":623}}}

At ba4366830 line 530 opens this.withTenantQueryRunner(data.tenantId, async (queryRunner) => { ... }) in handleUserDeleted; the twelve raw queryRunner.query statements it wraps (534, 538, 557, 585, 602, 611, 633, 649, 653, 660, 665, 670) filter on senderId / userId / messageId / channelId and none carries a tenantId predicate, so the adapter's literal sentence is accurate.
