    \"model\": \"claude-opus-5\",\n      \"prompt_hash\": null,\n      \"confidence\": 0.82,\n      \"rationale\": \"Anchor check at ba4366830: line 530 is the statement const objectKeysToPurge = await this.withTenantQueryRunner(...) inside handleUserDeleted; no queryRunner.query call begins on that line. The closure it opens (532-676) issues twelve raw statements against messages, channel_members, message_attachments, message_entity_references, message_analysis, message_reactions and message_receipts (534, 538, 557, 585, 602, 611, 633, 649, 653, 660, 665, 670), each predicated on senderId / userId / messageId / channelId and none on tenantId. The adapter's literal description therefore matches the code. What the rule asserts beyond the literal, a tenant-owned raw query executed without tenant scoping at severity high, is contradicted by behavior in the same file and in the DB layer it depends on. (1) withTenantQueryRunner (185-227) calls setTenantSchema (169-179), which rejects any tenantId that is not a UUID and pins search_path to the tenant clone schema first, so unqualified table names resolve to the clone holding only that tenant's rows. (2) It then calls bindTenantRlsContext (211), which in libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-transaction.ts:170-213 sets app.current_tenant, forces app.bypass_rls to off (185), reads both back and throws RLS_MISMATCH (202-212) if the binding does not hold; a stale pooled bypass cannot survive into this transaction. (3) The tenant_isolation_policy those GUCs drive is ENABLE plus FORCE row level security (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:527-532), FOR ALL with USING and WITH CHECK (547-553), so the UPDATE and DELETE statements in this cascade are fenced by tenantId = app.current_tenant, not only the SELECTs; FORCE covers the owner role the service connects as. (4) The policy is installed on the messaging source schema by the live baseline (1800000000000-Baseline.ts:82-85, excludeTables []) and on every tenant clone by the provisioner (schema-registry.ts:152-154 and 232-238 assign messaging tenantRls: true; tenant-schema-provisioner.ts:318-330 applies applyTenantRlsToSchema with schemaOverride), which is what closes the gap that CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL does not copy policies; runtime sync is off by design (app.module.ts:58, 358-360) because the provisioner owns clone DDL. (5) Every table the closure touches carries a tenantId column (message.entity.ts:50-51; message-attachment.entity.ts:32; message-receipt.entity.ts:37; message-reaction.entity.ts:33; message-analysis.entity.ts:73; message-entity-reference.entity.ts:50; channel_members is queried by tenantId at handler 245), so the helper's column-discovery arms the policy on each. (6) The adapter itself treats a /withTenant/i receiver or scope as tenant-scoped and suppresses the finding (tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:230, 254-264), but it evaluates that regex on enclosingFunctionLike (221-222, 472-481), the innermost arrow function 532-676, whose text does not include the identifier withTenantQueryRunner in the parent call at 530. The finding fired because of that scope-text choice, not because the queries escape tenant scoping; by the adapter's own intent this site is exempt. Residual observation, lower severity: the same file applies an explicit tenantId predicate in verifyMembership (243-250) and in the getMessageForBroadcast attachment and receipt reads (336-352) and its comment (236-242) names that predicate the second defense layer behind RLS; gdpr.service.ts does the same on its export reads (158-159, 196-218) while its erasure path (298-408) uses the same predicate-free shape as this closure. Adding AND tenantId = $N to the twelve statements would align the erasure cascade with that convention; the correct root-cause fix, if the operator wants the predicate made automatic rather than remembered per call, is to route these statements through a tenant-scoped repository or query helper that injects the predicate, not to rely on reviewers spotting omissions. That is a consistency improvement, not a high-severity tenant-isolation defect. Confidence is held at 0.82 rather than higher because the runtime DB role is not verified from this evidence set (RLS does not bind a superuser; the helper states the service connects as the owner aquaculture, which FORCE covers), because the anchor line 530 does not coincide with a query call at this SHA (the nearest is 534, consistent with a small line drift from an earlier revision that cannot be confirmed without git history), and because a stricter reading of the rule could retain a downgraded consistency finding. The supplied excerpt covered lines 1-220 and was insufficient for a finding anchored at 530, so the file was read directly at the snapshot; run_id, prompt_hash and judgment_group_id were not carried await this.withTenantQueryRunner(...) inside handleUserDeleted; no queryRunner.query call begins on that line. The closure it opens (532-676) issues twelve raw statements against messages, channel_members, message_attachments, message_entity_references, message_analysis, message_reactions and message_receipts (534, 538, 557, 585, 602, 611, 633, 649, 653, 660, 665, 670), each predicated on senderId / userId / messageId / channelId and none on tenantId. The adapter's literal description therefore matches the code. What the rule asserts beyond the literal, a tenant-owned raw query executed without tenant scoping at severity high, is contradicted by behavior in the same file and in the DB layer it depends on. (1) withTenantQueryRunner (185-227) calls setTenantSchema (169-179), which rejects any tenantId that is not a UUID and pins search_path to the tenant clone schema first, so unqualified table names resolve to the clone holding only that tenant's rows. (2) It then calls bindTenantRlsContext (211), which in libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-transaction.ts:170-213 sets app.current_tenant, forces app.bypass_rls to off (185), reads both back and throws RLS_MISMATCH (202-212) if the binding does not hold; a stale pooled bypass cannot survive into this transaction. (3) The tenant_isolation_policy those GUCs drive is ENABLE plus FORCE row level security (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:527-532), FOR ALL with USING and WITH CHECK (547-553), so the UPDATE and DELETE statements in this cascade are fenced by tenantId = app.current_tenant, not only the SELECTs; FORCE covers the owner role the service connects as. (4) The policy is installed on the messaging source schema by the live baseline (1800000000000-Baseline.ts:82-85, excludeTables []) and on every tenant clone by the provisioner (schema-registry.ts:152-154 and 232-238 assign messaging tenantRls: true; tenant-schema-provisioner.ts:318-330 applies applyTenantRlsToSchema with schemaOverride), which is what closes the gap that CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL does not copy policies; runtime sync is off by design (app.module.ts:58, 358-360) because the provisioner owns clone DDL. (5) Every table the closure touches carries a tenantId column (message.entity.ts:50-51; message-attachment.entity.ts:32; message-receipt.entity.ts:37; message-reaction.entity.ts:33; message-analysis.entity.ts:73; message-entity-reference.entity.ts:50; channel_members is queried by tenantId at handler 245), so the helper's column-discovery arms the policy on each. (6) The adapter itself treats a /withTenant/i receiver or scope as tenant-scoped and suppresses the finding (tenant-scoping-adapter.ts:230, 254-264), but it evaluates that regex on enclosingFunctionLike (221-222, 472-481), the innermost arrow function 532-676, whose text does not include the identifier withTenantQueryRunner in the parent call at 530. The finding fired because of that scope-text choice, not because the queries escape tenant scoping; by the adapter's own intent this site is exempt. Residual observation, lower severity: the same file applies an explicit tenantId predicate in verifyMembership (243-250) and in the getMessageForBroadcast attachment and receipt reads (336-352) and its comment (236-242) names that predicate the second defense layer behind RLS; gdpr.service.ts does the same on its export reads (158-159, 196-218) while its erasure path (298-408) uses the same predicate-free shape as this closure. Adding AND tenantId = $N to the twelve statements would align the erasure cascade with that convention; the correct root-cause fix, if the operator wants the predicate made automatic rather than remembered per call, is to route these statements through a tenant-scoped repository or query helper that injects the predicate, not to rely on reviewers spotting omissions. That is a consistency improvement, not a high-severity tenant-isolation defect. Confidence is held at 0.82 rather than higher because the runtime DB role is not verified from this evidence set (RLS does not bind a superuser; the helper states the service connects as the owner aquaculture, which FORCE covers), because the anchor line 530 does not coincide with a query call at this SHA (the nearest is 534, consistent with a small line drift from an earlier revision that cannot be confirmed without git history), and because a stricter reading of the rule could retain a downgraded consistency finding.
